MSCI 降評風險升高，印尼股市保守看待
2026 年 10 月 27 日
一、 市場動態
1. MSCI 擬將印尼降至獨立市場，降評風險明顯升高：MSCI 於 10 月 15 日宣布，啟動
將印尼股市由「新興市場（Emerging Market）」直接降至「獨立市場（Standalone
Market） 」的諮詢程序，意見徵詢將於2027 年 4 月 30 日截止，最晚於 2027 年 6 月
30 日公布最終決定 。MSCI 原先於今年 1 月提出可能降至 「邊境市場 （Frontier Market
） 」 ，此次則跳過「邊境市場」提出直接將該國股市降至「獨立市場」的方案，顯示對

印尼市場治理的疑慮進一步加深。雖然「邊境市場」仍可能是替代分類方案，但MSCI
強調，除非有明確證據顯示市場治理及運作出現實質改善，否則預期將依目前提案重
新分類，顯示此次降評風險較過去顯著升高。
2. 股權透明度與交易秩序問題為 MSCI 主要疑慮：MSCI 指出，印尼股市長期存在股權
結構不透明、實際自由流通股比例難以確認、疑似協調交易，以及市場監理執法成效
不足等問題，影響國際投資人對市場公平性與可投資性的信心。雖然印尼主管機關已
提出強化持股超過 1%股東資訊揭露、建立高持股集中度（HSC）監理機制，以及規
劃逐步將最低自由流通股比例提高至 15%等改革措施，但MSCI 認為相關改革尚未展
現持續且具實質性的改善成果。由於問題涉及市場制度與治理結構，改善所需時間可
能較一般短期市場波動更長。
3. 股市反彈後再度回落，外資撤離壓力仍高：受降評消息衝擊，印尼雅加達綜合指數 10
月 16 日重挫 11.57%，創 1998 年以來最大單日跌幅，19 日再跌 3.64%，盤中最低
觸及 4,907.37 點 。 雖然20 日至 23 日連續四個交易日反彈 ， 但26 日再度下跌 1.22%

，收於 5,373.09 點，各產業類股全面收黑，顯示市場信心仍不穩定。根據 Reuters
報導，截至 10 月 19 日，2026 年外資已累計淨賣超印尼股票約 48.8 億美元。此外，
印尼證交所 26 日宣布，將引進獨立專家及外國投資人代表參與高持股集中度審查，
並自 11 月起加強調查疑似協調交易，必要時凍結可疑帳戶，以回應 MSCI 對市場制
度的疑慮。
二、 下跌原因分析：
1. 股市：潛在指數剔除與資金外流，持續壓抑市場評價
若印尼股市最終降至「獨立市場」， 原則上將不再納入MSCI 新興市場及邊境市場相關
指數，追蹤相關指數的被動式基金將須依規則調整持股，實際處理方式仍以 MSCI 後
續公告為準。若改降至「邊境市場」，則可能獲得部分邊境市場資金承接，衝擊相對較
小。Reuters 引述多家金融機構估計，印尼股市降至獨立市場可能引發約 30 億至 60

策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕

億美元被動資金流出，其中 UBS 估計約 40 億美元，並指出主動型基金另有約 150 億
美元潛在撤資風險，惟實際規模仍視最終分類結果及基金策略而定。
2. 匯率：外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力
印尼股市的降評風險不僅衝擊股市，也可能透過外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力。印尼
盾 10 月 16 日盤中一度貶至 18,070 兌 1 美元，為 7 月下旬以來最弱水準之一，當日
收盤約 18,000。若後續國際資金持續流出，可能進一步增加股匯市波動，對以外幣計
價、且未完全避險的印尼基金及 ETF 形成額外壓力。雖然印尼央行可透過匯市干預及

流動性管理措施穩定金融市場，但在資金外流壓力尚未緩解前，印尼盾仍可能維持偏
弱格局。
3. 基金與 ETF：指數權重下滑，單一市場曝險風險升高
MSCI 相關指數調整已對印尼股票產生實質影響。Reuters 引用 LSEG 資料顯示，印尼
在 MSCI 新興市場指數的權重已由 2026 年初的 1.16%降至 9 月底的 0.43%，相關成
分股亦由年初 18 檔減至 9 檔，反映部分指數資金調整壓力已提前出現。對印尼單一
國家基金及 ETF 而言，主要風險來自股市下跌、資金流出及印尼盾波動；主動式基金
雖可透過選股降低部分影響，但仍難以完全迴避整體市場風險。不同產品實際受到的
影響，仍應依追蹤指數、主要持股及匯率避險安排個別評估。
三、 市場看法：
1. 制度性問題難以速解，降評風險將持續牽動市場：我們認為，此次 MSCI 降評風險並
非單純反映經濟成長放緩或企業獲利不佳，而是涉及市場透明度、交易公平性與監理
執法等結構性問題。尤其 MSCI 不僅啟動降評諮詢，更持續限制印尼股票的部分指數

調整，包括不提高外資納入因子、不新增相關成分股，以及不進行向上規模分類調整
，顯示市場可投資性疑慮尚未獲得有效解決。儘管政府試圖推動改革，但制度修正仍
須經過執行與成效驗證，才能重新取得國際投資人的信任，因此短期內降評風險仍難
完全消除。
2. 外資撤離與指數權重下降，市場信心修復恐需較長時間：印尼今年以來外資已大幅賣
超，MSCI 新興市場指數中的權重亦明顯下降，顯示國際投資人對當地市場的疑慮已
逐漸反映於實際資金流向。我們認為，即使印尼最終成功避免降評，部分國際機構投
資人仍可能因市場透明度、流動性及治理品質疑慮而維持較低曝險。此外，MSCI 對
部分指數調整的凍結措施尚未解除，短期內新增指數資金流入機會仍受到限制，預期
國際資金回流與市場評價修復均可能需要較長時間。
3. 監理改革力度加大，但尚不足以扭轉保守展望：印尼政府近期持續加強改革，包括提
高資訊透明度、強化交易監控、對違規交易開罰，以及引進外部獨立人士參與監理審
查。10 月 26 日該國政府進一步提出主動調查疑似協調交易及凍結可疑帳戶等措施，

11 月 2 日起亦規劃將高持股集中度股票排除於雅加達綜合指數之外 。 相關改革有助改

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
善交易秩序及短期市場信心，但部分措施仍待正式實施，且實際成效尚未獲得 MSCI
認可。考量制度性問題改善需要時間，政策利多雖可能帶動短線反彈，但尚不足以改
變對印尼股市中期前景的保守判斷。
4. 股市跌深不代表利空出盡，中期風險報酬吸引力偏低：印尼股市在 10 月 16 日及 19
日大幅修正後，雖曾連續四個交易日反彈，但 26 日再度回落，顯示市場尚未形成穩
定回升趨勢。我們認為，當前股市面臨的不僅是短期賣壓，更包括國際投資人信任下
降及風險溢價提高，即使企業基本面未同步惡化，仍可能因評價下修而承受股價壓力

。因此，短線不排除政策利多或跌深後出現技術性反彈，但在 MSCI 態度、外資流向
與印尼盾走勢明顯改善前，反彈延續性仍有疑慮，印尼股市中期展望維持保守。
四、市場展望：
1. 印尼股市維持保守展望，單一市場風險報酬吸引力不足：考量 MSCI 降評風險尚未解除、
外資撤離壓力持續 ， 以及市場治理改革仍有待驗證 ， 我們認為印尼股市未來數季的風險報
酬吸引力相對有限。即使近期股價明顯回落，仍難認定相關風險已充分反映。相較市場制
度成熟、國際資金流動性較佳的新興市場，印尼股市可能持續面臨較高風險溢價，因此對
單一印尼市場的集中曝險應維持審慎態度。
2. 此事件對新興市場指數與東協指數影響相對有限：根據 MSCI 資料，截至2026 年 9 月底
，印尼股市在 MSCI Emerging Market 指數與 MSCI AC ASEAN 指數中的權重分別約為
0.43%與 7.55%，因此我們認為印尼潛在降評對相關指數的直接衝擊相對有限。此外，新
興市場各國經濟結構與產業組成有所差異 ， 區域型基金可透過跨國分散降低單一市場風險

。惟後續仍須留意印尼事件對整體新興市場投資信心造成的外溢影響 ， 以及全球貿易與匯
率波動風險。

2026年 10月 30日 全8頁

Indicators Update 
2026年 9月雇用統計 
失業率は2.4％と前月から0.1％pt低下し、就業拡大が進展

経済調査部 エコノミスト 菊池 慈陽

[要約] 
◼ 2026年 9月の完全失業率（季節調整値）は 2.4％と、2カ月ぶりに低下（前月差▲
0.1％pt）し、7月の水準に戻った。失業者数は「自発的な離職」を中心に 2カ月ぶり
に減少（同▲10万人）し、就業者数は2カ月連続で増加（同＋34万人）した。労働参
加と就業拡大が進展し、雇用環境は前月から改善したとみられる。 
◼ 2026年9月の有効求人倍率（季節調整値）は1.19倍（前月差＋0.01pt）と3カ月ぶり
に上昇した。新規求職申込件数が大きく減少したことで、新規求人倍率は2.19倍（同
＋0.13pt）と上昇した。 
◼ 先行きの雇用環境は堅調に推移しよう。労働供給が中長期的に減少していく可能性が
高いこともあり、企業は高水準の賃上げなど、人材確保に向けた積極的な取り組みを
続けている。ただし、雇用環境の悪化リスクは小さくない。中東情勢をめぐる不確実
性は依然として高く、長期化する原油高が企業収益を悪化させ、幅広い産業で労働需
要を下押しする恐れがある。

9月の完全失業率：2.4％と前月から低下、「自発的な離職」による失業者が大きく減少 
2026年 9月の完全失業率（季節調整値）は2.4％と、2カ月ぶりに低下（前月差▲0.1％pt）
し、7月の水準に戻った（図表2左上） 。失業者数は2カ月ぶりに減少（同▲10万人）し、就業
者数は 2カ月連続で増加（同＋34万人）した。労働参加と就業拡大が進展し、雇用環境は前月
から改善したとみられる。 
失業者数を求職理由別に見ると、「自発的な離職」 （前月差▲13万人）が大きく減少し、全体
を押し下げた。「勤め先や事業の都合による離職」（同▲1万人）は小幅に減少した。他方、「定
年又は雇用契約の満了」（同＋1万人）と「新たに求職」（同＋1万人）は小幅に増加した（巻末
の雇用概況①下段左）。 
就業者数の前月からの変化を業種別に見ると、「製造業」と「医療，福祉」が大きく増加し、
全体を押し上げた。「卸売業，小売業」と「情報通信業」も増加した。他方、「建設業」やその
他の非製造業は減少した。（図表2左下）。 
雇用者数（役員を除く）を雇用形態別に見ると、正規雇用者は前月から 22万人増加した。
2026年春頃から増加傾向にあり、ピークだった2025年 11月の水準を上回った。非正規雇用者
は 5カ月ぶりに増加（前月差＋21万人）した（図表 2右下）。

3 / 8

9月の新規求人倍率：求職が大きく減少した一方で求人は増加し、前月から上昇 
2026年 9月の有効求人倍率（季節調整値）は 1.19倍（前月差＋0.01pt）と 3カ月ぶりに上
昇した。新規求人倍率は2.19倍（同＋0.13pt）と上昇した（図表3左）。 
求人側の動きを見ると、有効求人数は前月から横ばいだった一方、新規求人数（前月比＋
1.5％）は 2カ月ぶりに増加した（図表 4左） 。求職側では、有効求職者数（同▲1.4％）、新規
求職申込件数（同▲4.4％）はいずれも 3カ月ぶりに減少した。求職側は 2025年春頃から持ち
直しの動きが見られており、求人側も2026年春頃から増加傾向にある。 
雇用形態別に見ると、正社員の有効求人倍率（季節調整値）は1.01倍（前月差＋0.01pt）と
3カ月ぶりに上昇した。新規求人倍率も 1.79倍（同＋0.11pt）と 3カ月ぶりに上昇した（図表
3右）。

先行き：堅調な推移を見込むも、中東情勢など雇用環境の悪化リスクは小さくない 
先行きの雇用環境は、堅調に推移することを見込んでいる。労働供給が中長期的に減少して
いく可能性が高いこともあり、企業は高水準の賃上げなど1、人材確保に向けた積極的な取り組
みを続けている。 
日本銀行「全国企業短期経済観測調査」 （日銀短観）の2026年 9月調査では、雇用人員判断
DI（全規模全産業、 「過剰」－「不足」 ）は「最近」が▲38％ptと 6月調査から 1％pt低下し
（人手不足感が強まり）、「先行き」は▲41％ptと更なる低下が予想されている（巻末の雇用概
況①上段右）。特に、中小企業（全産業）は「最近」が▲42％ptと6月調査から3％pt低下し、
「先行き」は▲45％ptだった。中小企業を中心に、人手不足感が強まっているとみられる。 
株式会社ナウキャストによる「HRog賃金 Now」の求人数指数を見ると、正社員とパート・ア
ルバイトのいずれも 2026年春頃から上昇傾向にある（巻末の雇用概況③下段左）。人手不足感
の強まりを受けて、今後も労働需要は堅調に推移するとみられる。 
他方、トランプ米政権による高関税政策（トランプ関税）1F
2や、長期化する日中関係の冷え込
みは、引き続き労働需要の下押し要因となるだろう。 
また、雇用環境の悪化リスクは小さくない。中東情勢は依然として不確実性が高い状況が続
いており、とりわけ注意が必要だ。原油等の供給網の正常化は当面見込みにくく、輸入原油価
格が高止まりしている。原油高は原材料費や物流費などの上昇を通じて企業収益を悪化させ3、
幅広い産業で労働需要を下押しする可能性がある。

1 日本労働組合総連合会（連合）が公表した第 7回（最終）回答集計結果では、定期昇給相当込みの賃上げ率
（加重平均）が企業規模計で 5.01％と、前年（5.25％）をやや下回るものの、高水準を維持している。300人
未満の中小組合では 4.69％と前年（4.65％）をわずかながら上回り、中小企業の積極的な姿勢が目立った。詳
細は、日本労働組合総連合会（連合）「3年連続の 5％超え！～2026春季生活闘争 第7回（最終）回答集計結
果について～」（2026年7月3日）を参照。 
2 トランプ米政権は通商法 122条に基づく措置が失効する 7月 24日に新たな関税措置を発動した（日本経済新
聞 電子版「トランプ政権が新関税発動、日本 12.5％ 中間選挙へ看板政策継続」（2026年7月 24日））。 
3 原油高による物価の上昇については、神田慶司・畑中宏仁・中村華奈子・横田凱「日本経済見通し：2026年
8月」（大和総研レポート、2026年8月 24日）を参照。

### Page 1

ab 30 October 2026
Global Research
Key Call: MediaTek Inc.
Raising TPU guidance with stronger execution
TPU v9 on track to ramp from early 2028
Amid market skepticism on TPU execution, MediaTek delivered better outlook for Q4
and Cloud ASICs, as we previewed. Management raised TPU sales guidance from >US
$2bn to US$2.5bn for 2026, and from US$12-16bn to US$16bn for 2027. This reflects
the steady Google demand for v8t for training, critical to accelerate the new Gemini LLM

development. Importantly, for TPU v9, MediaTek re-affirmed it is on track for high
volume manufacturing in Q128, which clarified  recent noises around delayed tape out.
Our latest understanding is there may be two versions of MediaTek’s TPU v9: the first
version with 336G SerDes should be tape out soon in Nov 2026. There’s a second tape
out which is on track for delivery in Feb 2027. The second version features flexible
SerDes switch between 224G/336G for greater supply flexibility for the whole system.

Conclusion is, we think MediaTek is on good track to scale TPU  sales to our estimates of
US$44bn/53bn in 2028/29  with upside. See Figure 1U B S  f o r e c a s t s  f o r   M e d i a T e k ' s  T P U  b u s i n e s s for our TPU financial analysis.
Thoughts on the second Cloud ASIC client
MediaTek announced the 2nd Cloud ASIC client, which should contribute to revenue in
2028. MediaTek clarified it’s not a Chinese customer. We believe there are 2 higher

possibilities as what we flagged recently. 1) Tesla’s AI6 using Intel 14A, produced at
Terafab ramping in H228. Potential revenue opportunity on full year basis could be ~US
$2bn if MediaTek could ramp 1m units. Margin profile could be lower than TPU's 42%
GM given simpler design. 2) SpaceX/xAI's Dojo3. MediaTek should be partnering with
Nvidia to offer NVLink Fusion for faster time to market. Question is SpaceX/xAI's own
engineering resource and capability for Dojo3 after team reshuffle. If secured, our initial

estimate is revenue opportunity a year could be US$4-9bn, assuming good volume.
Margin profile may be similar or higher than TPU, in our view.
Meaningful operating margin expansion ahead
Q4 sales are guided to grow 28% QoQ at the mid point, beating consensus of 25%,
even with higher base from Q3. GM is guided to edge down to 45.0% at mid point, with
OPM meaningfully rising to 19.0% from 16.1% in Q3. As MediaTek starts ramping TPU
substantially into 2027, the key focus for profitability should shift from GM to OPM due

to different business model vs consumer electronics, and our sense is the market still
under appreciates the structural shift and the stronger earnings power. We forecast
MediaTek's OPM to rise to 25.2%/30.9% in 2027/28 vs consensus of 21.8%/24.2%.
Our 2027/28E EPS estimates are 23%/29% above consensus pre earnings.
Valuation: maintain Key Call Buy and target of NT$7,300
Among our coverage, MediaTek remains the one that we believe the market most

underestimates its execution and opportunity in Cloud AI. We raise 2026E EPS by 5%
and fine tune 2027/28E EPS by 1-2%. Maintain Key Call Buy. Our target of NT$7,300 is
based on 27x 2027-28E PE.

Company Description
MediaTek is a leading integrated circuit (IC) design company in Taiwan, focusing on solutions
from the edge to the cloud, applications for its product offerings range from smartphones,
smart homes and AI PCs to high-performance computing, automotive, and AI data centers.

Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our price target for MediaTek is based on a target PE methodology.
Fabless integrated circuit (IC) design houses face a variety of uncertainties, including rapidly
changing technology, end-market demand, competition, employee turnover and foundry
wafer costs. They are also vulnerable to legal disputes over intellectual property rights. In
addition, MediaTek is exposed to potential price competition in handset SoC, TV SoC, etc.

Downside risks include slower-than-expected market share gains and a weaker-than-
expected demand recovery.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

Valuation: We increase our 12m TP to NT$7,100 from NT$7,000 previously, following 
our upward earnings revisions. Our TP is based on a target P/E multiple of 25x 
(unchanged; +1.8 stdv above 5 yr avg. fwd P/E) applied to our FY2H27E-1H28E EPS 
(unchanged). We reiterate our Buy rating on MediaTek.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - MediaTek (2454.TW) 
Valuation: Our 12m TP of NT$7,100 is based on a target P/E multiple of 25x (1.8 stdv 
above its 5-year trading average) applied to our 2H27E-1H28E EPS. 
Key risks to our views: (1) Weaker-than-expected end demand especially with 
smartphones, (2) Higher foundry cost impacting its margin outlook, (3) Intensifying 
competition that would result in reduced profitability, and (4) Slower ramp in ASIC would 
result in changes in operating leverage

Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Chroma ATE (2360.TW)
05 November 2026 Citi Research
3
Chroma ATE
Valuation
Our target price of NT$2,900 is based on 40x 2027E P/E, at close to 1x PEG. Chroma shares have re-rated above its historical
range as its growth outlook is now strongly tied to AI infrastructure build out. We estimate 60%+ of its revenue are now AI-
related, with strong growth across product segments including power testers, SLT, metrology, and photonics. Chroma has a
commanding market share in most of these segments and well positioned to capitalized on the global AI expansion.

Risks
Downside risks to achieving our target price include: 1) softer AI/CPO growth expectation due to changes in capex outlook or
production bottleneck; 2) fewer SLT needed as a result of shortened cycle time as GPU yield rate improves; 3) disappointing
metrology shipment due to changes at ramp-up schedule; 4) testing process change leading to loss in market share; 5) FX risks
(50-60% of Chroma’s sales are in US$, 10-20% in Rmb).

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

Company Brief 
자동차/모빌리티 
Analyst 김준성 
joonsung.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 배현서 
hyeonseo.bae@meritz.co.kr 배당은 지켜질 것  
3Q26 지배주주 순이익, 컨센서스 -38% 하회 
매출 31.6조원 (+10% YoY) 으로 컨센서스에 부합했다. 출고 판매 (+10% YoY), 소
매 판매 (+8% YoY) 모두 높은 성장을 기록하며, 외형 확대에 기여했다. 
영업이익 1.82조원 (+24% YoY) 으로 컨센서스를 -25% 하회했다. 전년 동기 대미 
관세 25% 부과에 따른 기저효과 +5,330억원 (2025년 11월부터 15%로 하향)이 
존재했고 기말 환율 (원/달러 -12% QoQ 강세) 변동에 따른 판매보증 충당부채 환
입 +6,700억원을 인식했음에도 불구하고, 신규 출시 저가 BEV 모델들의 판매 확대
에 따른 전사 평균 판매가격 하락  할인 경쟁 심화  원재료 및 운송비 상승으로 부
진한 실적을 기록했다. 
지배주주 순이익 1.29조원 (-10% YoY) 으로 컨센서스를 -38% 하회했다. 영업 실적 
부진과 함께 원화 강세에 의한 외화자산 평가손실 및 관계사 실적 악화에 따른 지분
법 이익 감소 (-36% YoY)가 더해진 결과다.

6,000원 이상 배당 유지 전망 
4Q26 이후 실적 방향도 먹구름이 가득하다. 원/달러 환율의 YoY 강세 반영이 시작
된다. 미국 시장 내 경쟁력 확보를 위해 가장 중요한 원/엔 환율의 방향성 또한 비우
호적 흐름이 지속 중이다. 내수 시장을 포함해 전세계 주요 시장 내 중국산 차량과의 
경쟁 강도도 높아지고 있다. 원유 가격 뿐만 아니라 알루미늄, 구리 등 핵심 원재료 
가격의 상승 반영도 이어지고 있다. 
2026년 연간 지배주주 순이익 6.9조원 (-9% YoY) 을 전망한다. 주주환원 총액은 축
소될 것이다. 다만 자사주 매입을 줄이더라도 배당은 전년 수준을 지킬 것으로 기대
한다. 참고로 2025년 실적에 기반한 주주환원은 자사주 매입 0.9조원, 배당 2.6조원 
(주당 6,800원)이었으며, 주주환원율은 48%였다. 현대차그룹은 Boston Dynamics
에 대한 증자를 이어가고 있다. 정의선 회장도 마찬가지다. 그리고 기아의 배당은 현
대차  현대글로비스의 배당과 함께 증자 재원으로 활용되고 있다. 6,000 원 이상의 
배당 유지를 기대하며, 6% 이상 시가 배당 수익률 제시를 통해 주가 하방 경직성 확
보가 가능하다고 판단한다. 기업가치 상승 재개를 위해서는 기존 차량 제조  판매 사
업을 넘어 피지컬 데이터 디바이스 제공 사업 진출 가시성 확보가 필요하다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 11. 2

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 150,000원 
현재주가 (10.30) 95,600원 
상승여력 56.9%

주가 추이

3Q26 매 출액 과 영 업 이익 은 비현 금 성 비용 항목 개 선 으로 시장 기대치
를 상 회 했 으나, 차량 판매 감소와 인 플 레 이션 등으로 연간 가이 던 스 는 
하 향 되 었 습니 다. 올해 생산 차질 에 따라 이 연된 신차 판 매를 ’27년에 
확대하며 매출/이익 회복을 가시 화 하 는 부분 에 주목 필 요 합니다. 
매출 성장은 금융부문이, 영업이익은 자동차부문이 기여 
동사 3Q26 매출액은 45.1조원(-3.5% 이하 Y oY), 영업이익 2.68조원
(+5.5%)을 기록하며 시장 기대치를 상회했다. 파업으로 인한 생산 차질
로 3Q 판매량 92.1만대(-11.3%)에 그치며 자동차부문 매출은 큰 폭으
로 감소(33조원, -10.0%)했으나, 금융부문 매출이 취급 자산 증가로 큰 
폭으로 성장(+27.8%)했다. 반면, 영업이익은 금리 상승에 따른 금융부문 
영업비용 확대 영향과 기타부문 실적 부진 발생과 달리 자동차부문은 전
년 동기 대비 관세율 차이에 따른 비용 감소와 판매보증 충당부채 전입
액 감소 등 우호적 환율효과로 큰 폭의 증가세(+24.8%) 기록했다. 
’26년 가이던스 하향, 구조적 반등 요인은 아직 
3Q 실적 발표에서 주목할 부분은 가이던스 하향과 현금흐름이다. 연간 
판매목표를 연초 415.8만대에서 400만대로 하향했고 영업이익률 목표
는 6.3~7.3%에서 5.5~6.5%로 0.8%p 하향했으며, ’26년 투자계획은 
17.8조원에서 16조원으로 낮췄다. 특히, 투자계획 하향은 차량 판매 매
출 감소 대비 인센티브, 원자재 가격 상승 등으로 현금흐름이 악화된 
부분에 기인한 것으로 판단된다. 실적발표에서 전사적 원가 최적화 활
동 추진을 강조했으나, 이는 좀 더 장기적 측면에서 효과가 가시화될 
것으로 판단되며, 인플레이션 및 원화절상 상황 고려 시 구조적 반등은 
단기적으로 쉽지 않을 수 있다. 
절치부심의 2027년, 목표주가 600.000원 유지 
그러나 매크로 상황을 제 외 하 면  ’26년 하반기 생산 차질에 따른 신차 
판매효과가 ’27년 으 로 이연된 것 이 라 는 점에 주목 필요하다.   ‘북미 HEV, 유럽 
BEV’ 전략 하에서 엘란트라, 투싼 풀세인지, GV80, G80 등 고수익 HEV 차종 
중심 미국 수요 대응을 통해 Mix 개선하는 모습이 확인되고, 생산이 본 격 화 된 
아이오닉3 유럽 판매 확대 시 중국 로 컬 업 체 들 의 공세를 방 어 하 는 모 습 을 
확인하는 것이 가 능 할 것으로 판 단 한 다. 노 후 차 종 중심 운 영 에 따른 판매 둔화 
이외에 아직 근 원 적 판매 경쟁력 저하로는 판단하지 않는다.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10월 30일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 6

현대차 (005380) [한화리서치]

6 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일)

### Page 1

한화에어로스페이스 (012450)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 방위산업 | 2026. 11. 2  
방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실적 급감

방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실적 급감 
지상방산 매출 1조 2,450억원으로 전년비 41%, 영업이익 217억원으로 전년비 
96.2% 급감하며 영업이익률은 1.7% 기록. 실적 급감에 대해 사측은 올해 주요 사업
의 납품 일정이 4분기에 집중됨에 따라 3분기 인도 물량이 적었던 것과 대전 사업장 
생산 중단에 따른 영향 때문이라고 설명. 단, 4분기에는 당초 계획에 따라 주요 사업의 
납품 물량이 집중되어 있어 실적이 개선될 것이고 대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 내에 정
상 가돌 하는 것이 목표라고 밝힘 
수주잔고 증가세 지속으로 성장 지속 가능할 것 
3분기 말 수주잔고 35조원으로 전년비 12.9% 증가. 이를 통한 지상방산 부문 성장 
지속 가능할 것으로 판단. 참고로 사측은 내년 매출 15% 수준 성장 전망 유지. 하지
만 금리 상승에 따른 글로벌 업종 평균 밸류에이션 하락에 따른 방산 부문 적용 멀티
플 하향 조정 그리고 상장 연결회사(한화오션, 한화시스템, 쎄트렉아이)와 상장 비연결
회사(한국항공우주)의 주가 하락 반영 영향으로 목표주가는 하향 조정 
8월 미 육군 차륜형 자주포 사업에서 시제 사업자로 선정. 향후 K9MH는 미 육군의 
작전 운용 개념에 맞춰 맞춤 조정을 거친 후에 특별한 결함 혹은 결격 사유가 없으면 
최종 계약 체결 예정. 동시에 미 육군의 궤도형 자주포 사업에도 참여할 계획. 뿐만 아
니라 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등으로 신시장 개척 역시 지속되고 있다는 점도 긍
정적 
 Analyst 이재광 
rheejaekwang @lssec.co.kr

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Stronger Growth, Costlier Money
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The latest GDP report and annual revisions show a sturdier U.S. economy than previously
reported. According to the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA), real GDP grew at a 2.2%
annualized pace in Q2 compared to 1.5% previously estimated, and Q1 was revised
from 2.1% to 2.5%. Both 2024 and 2025 were also revised slightly higher. Most of the

first half of the year revision was in categories related to the Artificial Intelligence (AI)
buildout, but real consumer spending has also been revised slightly higher, accelerating
from 0.7% annualized growth in Q1 to 3.8% in Q2 despite surging energy prices and
slow hiring.
Spending in July and August remained much stronger than labor market conditions alone
would suggest, also averaging 3.7% annualized growth. Upside revisions to net interest

income and personal saving suggest households have benefited from more income than
was previously apparent, with personal saving revised higher back to 2023. The new data,
according to the BEA, show the January to July 2026 savings rate averaged 4.8% versus
3.3% prerevision, strengthening consumer spending underpinnings despite moderate
hiring, high energy prices and rising borrowing costs.
The BEA update also included inflation methodological changes related to categories

inflated by the AI boom—such as portfolio management fees and computer software and
accessories. These improved the inflation picture to some extent—August “core” PCE
inflation was reduced by 0.3 ppts to 3% YoY, bringing it closer to “core” consumer price
index inflation, which was 2.5% YoY and 2% on a three-month annualized basis. While still
above the 2% Federal Reserve (Fed) target, “core” PCE now appears stable at 3% year over
year since January rather than accelerating. These methodological changes are expected

to dampen pass-through of AI-related price pressures into “core” inflation and effects of
stock-market swings on inflation.
They also produced an encouraging improvement in recent PCE inflation momentum.
Three-month annualized “core” PCE inflation slowed from 4.3% at the peak of tariff
effects in February to 2.1% in August. For durable goods, this rate was down from 5.8%
in February to 1.5% in August, and, for services ex-energy and healthcare, it slowed from

4.2% to 2.2%, for example. Inflation is volatile on a three-month change basis, so it
remains to be seen whether this moderation continues without further Fed tightening.
For now, the trends in the San Francisco Fed’s cyclical versus acyclical inflation measures
diverged again in August. As expected, given softening wage growth, prices most sensitive
to the labor market have eased further. At 3% YoY, this inflation category is down from
8% in early 2023 and 4.6% two years ago is back to prepandemic levels and is likely

to ease more (Exhibit 1A). In contrast, supply bottlenecks, high energy prices and other
industry-specific factors have kept acyclical inflation on an uptrend. As shown in Exhibit
1B, the relentless increase in freight costs could prolong upside pressure on this category.
The diesel market has tightened in recent months, boosting its price by 70% YoY,
consistent with Brent oil at around $200 per barrel and large reverberations across
a wide swath of goods and services prices. A government-policy-related decline in

available truck drivers is also raising transportation costs more than expected. So are
tight ocean shipping capacity and longer/more expensive voyages due to the U.S./Iran
War. Supply-chain stress also increased as companies rushed to front-run potential new
tariff increases.
On the positive side for the inflation outlook, unit labor costs (ULC) are up modestly
as robust productivity growth has absorbed much of the wage growth, limiting a major
source of domestic inflation. Upside productivity growth revisions are likely to further

reduce ULC, which could help shift focus from energy and AI-related inflation to
eventual disinflationary effects once oil and refined fuel prices start to ease. Sustained
productivity gains would support growth and margins while limiting inflation and the
need for much higher interest rates.
Investment Implications
We favor selective Equity exposure
to companies with durable demand
and pricing power, while remaining
cautious about businesses most

exposed to fuel, freight, and
financing costs. We would also
gradually increase long-duration
exposure.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Surging nominal growth has boosted corporate revenues (up 13% YoY in Q2, according to the
BEA) while contained unit labor costs helped raise margins and profits more than expected.
With labor costs declining as a share of revenues, investment-related tax incentives and
rock-bottom net interest expense, pre-tax nonfinancial corporate profits as measured in GDP
accounts rose 21% YoY in Q2. Total corporate profits after-tax were up 18.2% YoY, according to

the BEA.
Surging AI-related imports and investment are keeping the business and profits cycle on
an uptrend both here and abroad. According to BofA Global Research, the global economic
cycle backdrop improved further in September, as reflected in a broad-based increase in its
Global Wave1 indicator. Broadening U.S. manufacturing strength, fueled by ongoing AI-driven
investment, a healthier orders backlog and low inventories suggest that the expansion has

room to run—a constructive environment for earnings. Typically, a Global Wave upswing is
consistent with rising earnings revisions ratios and equity price uptrends. According to the
Global Wave, global earnings growth expectations remain exceptionally strong.
Still, absent a resolution of war-related energy and transportation disruptions, the same
industrial strength intensifies inflation pressures. A prolonged supply shock raises the risk of
restrictive interest rates and weakening economic growth. In this context, the surge in the

10-year Treasury yield above 5% is starting to reflect more than optimism about U.S. growth
and strengthening productivity outlook. Oil’s tightest recent correlation with 10-year Treasury
yields in 35 years suggests a common energy-price thread behind rising yields in developed
countries.
Bond market spillovers from normalizing Japan yields and capital repatriation are another
common thread. Higher yields on foreign sovereign bonds such as in Japan—which has

escaped stagnation and deflation by intensely pursuing investment-led growth—weaken the
relative appeal of Treasurys. That distinction matters for portfolios. A yield increase driven
mainly by durable productivity gains would come with a stronger outlook for future income
and earnings. A yield increase driven by cost shocks and greater uncertainty raises financing
costs without the same benefit.
In sum, the economy remains more resilient than expected, but the bond market is also

charging more for an uncertain inflation outlook given untethered government debt and
cooling global demand for Treasurys spurred by geopolitical tensions and Japan capital
repatriation. The case for much further tightening becomes stronger if wage growth
strengthens, productivity lags, and unit labor costs reaccelerate, causing cyclical inflation to
also pick up steam. The case becomes weaker if that measure stays contained while fuel and
freight pressures recede or rising Treasury yields reduce asset prices and economic growth.

Exhibit 1: Softer Cyclical/Higher Acyclical Inflation Keeps “Core” PCE at 3% Year Through August.
A) Cooling wage pressures ease cyclical inflation (housing, food services and accommodation,
etc.).
B) Surge in U.S. AI-related imports, tariff front-running, shipping disruptions and fuel- price
spike feed acyclical inflation.

4 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Yields, Yen and Iran: What Haunts the “House”
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
We recently highlighted the three-front challenge facing Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent:
engineering a stronger yen, containing the 10-year Treasury yield and securing a diplomatic
resolution to the conflict with Iran.

Bessent recently has delivered one of the year’s more memorable lines while discussing
Treasury policy and the yen: “I have asymmetric information. I am the house now.” 2 Yet several
challenges remain: the 10-year Treasury yield is near levels last seen in the early 2000s, the
yen remains structurally weaker, and a phased agreement to reopen the Strait of Hormuz
appears remote. The resulting strain on energy markets keeps inflation risks elevated.

Put differently, oil prices, Treasury yields and the dollar-yen exchange rate are all trending
against Bessent’s objectives. Below, we examine how each variable is haunting his “house.”
Haunting the “House” is the Yen. Since the U.S. and Japan mounted a record $98.7B
coordinated intervention in late July after the yen weakened to a 40-year low near 164 per
dollar, the currency has appreciated roughly 4% against the dollar. The move was further
supported on September 18 when the BoJ raised rates by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%,

their highest level since the mid-1990s (Exhibit 2A). However, the split 7–2 vote and Governor
Ueda’s limited forward guidance leave the path of further tightening uncertain. The key
question is whether additional BoJ hikes can produce sustained appreciation in the yen, or
merely reinforce the temporary relief provided by the summer intervention.
Bessent, meanwhile, has repeatedly signaled his preference for a stronger yen. Partly because
yen stability is also a U.S. financing concern. Persistent currency weakness could prompt

Japan to support its currency by selling foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys—which would
be an unwelcome prospect as Washington partially relies on foreign capital flows to fund its
large fiscal deficits. That potential “haunts” the Treasury Secretary.  At precisely the moment
Washington needs foreign capital to finance its enormous fiscal deficits, it would be inopportune
for one of America’s largest creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities.

Haunting the “House” are Yields. The surge in yields runs counter to a White House intent
on lowering borrowing costs. The benchmark 10-year yield has broken above the 5% threshold
and climbed to levels not seen since 2007. At around 5.30%, it stands more than a full
percentage point above where it began the year (Exhibit 2B). The level is striking but so is the
speed of the move: Stronger-than-expected economic data, including a survey showing U.S.

2 September 8, 2026.

5 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
business activity expanded at the fastest pace in more than five years, helped trigger an 18
bps surge in the 10-year yield—among its largest daily moves in decades. The 5-year yield also
topped 5% for the first time since 2007, while the 30-year yield reached 5.63%, its highest
level since 2002.
Compounding the move is inflation, running above the Fed’s 2% target for 67 months,

large fiscal deficits and the prospect of continued growth in Treasury issuance. Against this
backdrop, Treasury bought back $4.1 billion of long-dated debt, versus a maximum of $6
billion. That effort has done little to arrest the selloff. A subsequent auction priced nearly $4
billion of 7-year notes at 5.085%, the highest yield for that maturity in 33 years.
All in, the MOVE index, a key gauge of Treasury market volatility, jumped from 78 to 95 on

Wednesday September 23 and then to 104 the following day, its highest level since the early
days of the Iran war (Exhibit 3A).
Haunting the “House” is the Iran conflict. Early expectations that military pressure and
economic sanctions would quickly bring Iran to the negotiating table have yet to materialize.
Iranian officials have signaled that a full reopening of the Strait of Hormuz remains unlikely
while sanctions stay in place. Although talks between Washington and Tehran are continuing,

conflicting signals and lingering skepticism from both sides have kept tensions elevated.
As a result, oil prices have remained elevated in a range even as some Middle East supply
disruptions began to ease.
Startling is that Brent crude is up more than 60% this year, but refined product prices
have risen even faster. Ongoing disruptions to energy flows through the Strait of Hormuz,
combined with Ukrainian strikes on Russian refining infrastructure have tightened global

supplies of crude oil and refined fuels. Saudi Arabia has restored some drone-disrupted flows,
providing roughly 3.5 million barrels a day of relief (roughly 3% of global consumption).
The squeeze is mostly acute in diesel markets, where inventories remain constrained and
prices continue to climb (Exhibit 3B). U.S. diesel prices recently surpassed $6.50 per gallon,
the highest level on record, just as Russia has extended restrictions on diesel exports into

October. Against this backdrop, proposals to ban U.S. fuel exports risks worsening the
imbalance. According to S&P Global, an export ban could reduce refinery runs by nearly 2
million barrels per day and significantly cut gasoline production, potentially driving gasoline
and jet fuel prices even higher. As long as tensions persist, the oil impulse remains a key
impediment to structurally lower inflation.
Investment implications. Treasury Secretary Bessent increasingly sits at the intersection of

monetary policy, fiscal policy, foreign policy and national security. We remain constructive on
the U.S. economy and overweight U.S. Equities but are watching as these fronts challenge the
narrative. There is a great deal riding on the “house” versus the key macro variables of today.

6 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
When It Comes to Attracting Foreign Capital, the U.S. Remains Exceptional
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
There may be no better vote of confidence in a country than the willingness of
foreigners to put their money there. And by that measurement, the U.S. remains the
exception—both portfolio and FDI inflows are currently running at record highs based

on the latest data. This dynamic is one key reason why we remain constructive on U.S.
assets over the longer term.
Notwithstanding all the handwringing about America’s fiscal deficits, tariffs and rising
government debt, global investors sunk some $942B in net purchases of U.S. Equities in
the 12 months to July, the highest rolling 12-month total on record. In Q2, net purchase
of U.S. Equities soared to $426B, up 62% from the same period a year ago (Exhibit 4A).

By country, flows were down from China—not surprisingly—but strong from Taiwan and
South Korea, who have taken to recycling their massive AI-generated trade surplus back
into the U.S. In the end, strong portfolio inflows suggest that international investors
remain willing to increase their exposure to the world's largest capital market—that’s
bullish for U.S. assets.
Meanwhile, Exhibit 4B highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be

inside the U.S., with FDI inflows totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year.
That is a staggering 103% jump from a year ago and means FDI inflows are on track for
a record year, estimated at $480B for 2026.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Two, within the $32 trillion-plus economy with extraordinarily consumer

purchasing power, foreign firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment
than just trade (exports). Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers,
semiconductors, power generation, etc., has created numerous potential investment
opportunities for many foreign firms that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging
capex cycle. Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly
developed technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.

In the end, double-barreled capital inflows speak to the absolute and relative
attractiveness of the U.S. economy and capital markets. Stay invested in America—the
rest of the world is.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

ICE BofA MOVE Index is a market-implied measure of U.S. bond market volatility.
Consumer Price Index measures the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a market basket of consumer goods and services.
Baltic Dry Index is a daily shipping and trade index published by the Baltic Exchange in London that measures the cost of moving raw materials like iron ore, coal, and grain by sea.
Volatility Index (VIX) is a real-time market indicator that measures the stock market's expected volatility over the next 30 days.

This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.

oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.

of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

### Page 1

A delayed reaction to the Democrat blue wave has seen some
powerful selling of tech stocks. A little surprisingly, the reaction
in FX markets has remained very muted. FX participants so far
see this as an idiosyncratic re-pricing of the tech sector, rather
than a harbinger of recession. Expect defensive FX to
outperform, rather than a broad dollar decline

Chris Turner, Frantisek Taborsky and Francesco Pesole
The Israeli shekel is particularly sensitive to swings in US tech stocks
USD: Payrolls can provide the dollar
some support
We have all seen big falls in tech stocks on the back of the threat to
data centres. The fact that the equity sell-oﬀ spread to the
defensive sectors of consumer staples, utilities and healthcare
serves as a warning that an asset management industry
underweight in cash may be tidying up positions into year-end.
After all, most global benchmark equity indices, apart from China,
are still in the green on a year-to-date basis.

### Page 2

FX markets have reacted to the equity sell-oﬀ in a typically
defensive, but muted way. In the G10 space, the high beta
Australian and New Zealand dollars are underperforming, while the
defensive Japanese yen and Swiss franc are in demand. However,
the moves have been small. So far, it seems FX participants are
prepared to see the tech sell-oﬀ as an idiosyncratic story on the
back of the Democrat blue wave, rather than a major macro
setback. Notably, the Philadelphia semiconductor index trades on a

61% one-month implied volatility compared to just 7% for USD/JPY.
This may help investors to see FX markets as a less correlated asset
class compared to the wild swings in tech.
Where we have seen the highest correlations in FX are places like
the Israeli shekel. Local investors run high FX hedge ratios on their
US investments and will now be in the process of buying back dollar
hedges on their shrinking US tech portfolios.
The big question for the dollar is whether this tech sell-oﬀ leads to a

broader re-assessment of the US growth and inﬂation trajectory. US
short-dated rates have fallen close to 50bp since late September
and the dollar's yield advantage over other major pairs has
softened a little. Feeding into that story today will be the October
US jobs report. Second-tier US job indices this week have all come in
on the strong side and there seems to be no reason to expect a soft
jobs ﬁgure today. The whisper number currently stands at +82k

versus last month's soft reading of +29k.
A decent jobs number today will conﬁrm the Fed's understanding
that the US jobs market is 'solid' and could provide some support to
short-dated rates and the dollar. We still think it is too soon to call a
broad-based dollar decline and that defensive positions in FX
markets will be expressed through cross rates like a lower AUD/JPY
or in the emerging world with a weaker South African rand or
Mexican peso (see below).

The DXY dollar index is being remarkably resilient and can continue
to trade towards the middle of a 101.50-102.50 range. Clearly, the
dollar's liquidity advantages and near 4% one-week deposit rates
are helping here.

### Page 3

Chris Turner
EUR: Unimpressive
It has been a rather unimpressive performance from the euro. We
know that the European buy-side is running low hedge ratios on its
US investments. Yet a large US tech sell-oﬀ is still not enough to
prompt much EUR/USD buying at all. It seems that global investors
are more than happy to leave their cash in dollars for the time
being. In other words, we are seeing a rotation into cash and not
out of the dollar. Helping that story is the return to more familiar

correlations, where US Treasuries are now performing their safe
haven role.
Barring a soft US jobs report today, expect EUR/USD to consolidate
not far from 1.12. On the eurozone calendar today is ECB Chief
Economist Philip Lane at 10:30 CET. The market still prices 18bp of
ECB hikes in December and that should remain sticky unless we see
a much deeper equity correction.
A hawkish Bank of England can see sterling continue to outperform.
The prospect of UK inﬂation heading up towards 5% in the second

quarter next year makes BoE hawkish pricing quite resilient.
EUR/GBP has overshot rate diﬀerentials down at these low 0.83
levels, but there seems to be no reason to expect an upside
correction just yet.
Chris Turner
CEE: NBP and CNB have swapped roles
The main takeaway from the Czech National Bank meeting
yesterday was the 4-3 split within the board, following a long
period of a strong majority. The market interprets this as a dovish
signal, suggesting the CNB is unwilling to hike rates further unless

the inﬂation situation deteriorates signiﬁcantly. For the ﬁrst time
since the onset of the US-Iran conﬂict, the CNB’s forecast aligns
closely with market pricing, with the PRIBOR rate peaking at around
4.55%. However, inﬂation is expected to worsen over the remainder
of the year, approaching 3% and nearing 4% in 27Q1. We do not
believe the current shift in energy prices justiﬁes such a rally in CZK

The National Bank of Poland press conference marked a shift from a

accumulating and that the MPC is becoming more hawkish. This

aligns with our baseline scenario, which anticipates a rate hike in
the ﬁrst quarter of 2027. However, PLN rates underperformed
relative to the rest of the region yesterday. It appears the NBP
remains sensitive to the GDP outlook, with government-imposed
price caps set to be a key factor.
For now, it is clear that the zloty may receive some support from
the NBP’s shift toward a more hawkish stance. Nevertheless, global
factors remain the primary driver; yesterday’s trading saw a rapid

currencies. EUR/PLN is expected to settle somewhere within the
4.400–4.410 range.

225bp – the lowest in decades. Banxico is clearly focused on weak

Document 350: Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

Jan Hatzius 
+1(212)902-0394 | jan.hatzius@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC
Global Views: Downside Growth Risks
26 October 2026 | 6:45AM EDT
1. So far in 2026, global growth has been remarkably unremarkable.  Both the world 
as a whole and the G3 economies are growing in line with our estimates of potential 
GDP .  Our interpretation is that fiscal easing and the positive impulse from the AI 
boom have offset the negative impulse from the war in Iran and the lingering drag 
from higher US tariffs.

2. Our standing global GDP forecasts project more of the same in 2027, as lower 
energy prices offset fading tailwinds from fiscal policy and AI.  But the balance of 
risks is on the downside.  The most immediate concern is that, far from subsiding, 
energy prices have again climbed sharply on fears of renewed Middle East escalation.  
The prices of gasoline, diesel, jet fuel, and European/Asian natural gas are now near 
or even above their March/April highs, with likely negative effects on consumers and 
energy-importing economies more broadly.

### Page 2

3. Although we don’t expect significant fiscal policy changes in 2027, the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—have 
started to show up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates.  The moves have 
been mostly orderly, but the increase since early 2026 averages a sizable 100bp across 
DM yield curves.  If sustained, we estimate that this increase will subtract a cumulative 
0.6% from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment.  
(Only part of the drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect bond 
yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.)

Exhibit 2: Oil Price Risks Are Skewed to the Upside

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 3

4. The growth impulse from the AI boom was set to peak in late 2026 or early 2027 even 
before concerns around the sustainability of the AI investment boom intensified in 
recent weeks.  Although AI investment is still rising rapidly, we expect the increase as a 
share of GDP to slow over the next year.  Alongside the fading boost from last year’s 
increase in US depreciation allowances, this will weigh on capital spending growth, 
especially in the US.  AI has also boosted US consumer spending growth via a positive 
wealth effect worth an estimated 0.4pp (or 0.25% of GDP) over the past year.  This 
impact is likely to decline over the next year, gradually if stocks perform in line with our 
strategists’ reasonably upbeat expectations but more sharply if AI-related stocks 
continue to decline.

5. Our own base case remains that AI investment will ultimately pay off in terms of higher 
productivity and economywide profits.  However, one key channel for this payoff is labor 
market disruption, which has negative near-term macroeconomic effects.  And while DM 
labor markets still look solid in aggregate, we are starting to see clearer signs that the AI 
impact is spreading beyond the technology sector.  Across nonmanufacturing industries, 
the share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment.

6. The Fed’s recent signals—explicit from Williams/Jefferson and implicit from Warsh—
effectively rule out a hike on October 28.  But the FOMC still looks very likely to deliver 
another 25bp hike to 4-4.25% on December 9 because spot inflation remains too high.  
And while we continue to expect significant disinflation next year on the back of 
reduced impulses from tariffs, energy prices, and AI-related prices, the risks to our 
forecast are on the upside, most obviously in energy.  Higher inflation could result in

another hike in March, but either way we see renewed cuts in late 2027/early 2028.

7. Like the Fed, the ECB will likely hold rates steady next week and deliver a final hike (to 
2.75%) at the December meeting.  However, the turbulence in the French bond market 
poses somewhat greater downside risks to that call than in the US.  The turbulence has 
tightened financial conditions to a degree that is likely to shave ¼-½pp from annualized 
growth in coming quarters.  And if it intensifies into December, markets might perceive a 
hike as signaling insufficient focus by the ECB on the risk of Euro area fragmentation.  
While the ECB could address this issue by coupling a hike with renewed balance sheet 
expansion, the hurdle for such a move is high, in part because purchases of French 
bonds would inevitably raise concerns about moral hazard with respect to the needed 
fiscal adjustment.

November 5.  Consistent with our view that the UK economy is stronger than widely 
perceived, GDP growth has surprised on the upside this year and the unemployment rate 
declined to 4.7% in August on a 3-month average basis (with the volatile 1-month rate 
plunging to 4.1%).  Rising inflation and the looming household energy price reset in 
January imply a significant risk of further moves, although market pricing of 4+ hikes 
(including November) still looks excessive.  We also expect hikes in Canada and New 
Zealand this week and in Australia next week.

9. Slightly faster than expected growth in Q3 has pushed up our 2026 GDP forecast for 
China to 4.6%.  But most activity indicators are much weaker.  Both property activity and 
fixed asset investment are contracting year-on-year, and while nominal retail sales 
growth picked up to +1.1% in September, large retailers (whose sales are relatively 
better measured) are still posting substantial year-on-year declines.  With domestic 
demand rising slowly at best, sustained GDP growth in the official 4.5-5% target range 
will require ongoing steep increases in the trade surplus.  But the surplus is already at 1% 
of global GDP , and further increases will eat more noticeably into output and 
employment abroad.  Ultimately, China’s trading partners may respond by imposing 
trade barriers that weigh on Chinese exports and GDP , although the measures taken so 
far have been limited.

Exhibit 8: UK Growth Forecasts Have Drifted Higher

Source: Bloomberg, Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   6
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 7

10. Our strongest global view is that central banks will deliver fewer hikes than currently 
priced.  In addition, our rates strategists flag underweight positioning, diminished 
technical risks, and risk asset pressures as reasons why the recent rally might extend.  
The market’s narrow breadth makes our equity strategists wary of momentum factor 
exposure, but they still expect the S&P 500 to resume its climb after the midterm 
election, as earnings continue to grow solidly.  Our credit strategists remain cautious on 
IG technology bonds but see scope for some near-term reversal in HY given the scale of 
the recent selloff.  Our FX strategists expect the Japanese yen to act as a hedge against 
further AI jitters, in part because such jitters would shrink US-Japan interest rate 
differentials.  And our commodity strategists recommend targeted exposures to oil 
products and European natural gas to hedge near-term escalation risks alongside 
longer-term exposures to copper, gold, and select power markets to benefit from 
structural supply/demand imbalances. 
Jan Hatzius

Document 351: Gridlock Won & Markets Cheer

Source PDF: all report pdf/Gridlock Won & Markets Cheer.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Gridlock Won & Markets Cheer
Date:    6 hours ago
Gridlock Won & Markets Cheer

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Gridlock Won & Markets Cheer
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 4 Nov 2026
View in browser

I. Why Wall Street Is Cheering the Blue Sweep
Ahead of Tuesday's midterm elections, we warned that a Democratic
sweep of Congress could deliver a cold shower to Wall Street. It still could,
but it didn't do so today. Democrats captured both chambers of Congress,
flipping seven Republican-held Senate seats, including historic victories in
Texas, Kansas, and South Carolina. Financial markets put a positive spin
on the blue wave.
Oil prices fell on hopes that the US and Iran are more likely now to resolve
their war diplomatically. If so, then the outlook for inflation will improve. So
the Fed might not have to raise the federal funds rate as much. The

### Page 2

federal deficit might not widen further if Democrats refuse to increase
defense spending, while President Donald Trump vetoes their attempts to
provide Medicare for all. If Democrats slow the AI buildout, hyperscalers'
profits and free cash flow will increase. Fewer AI-related bond issues
would also help keep a lid on bond yields.
Initially, Nasdaq-100 futures plunged as much as 2.4% overnight, while
S&P 500 futures fell 1.4%. But the selloff reversed after Trump struck a

surprisingly conciliatory tone, congratulating Democrats and saying he
was willing to work with them.
That is when the cheer on Wall Street started. The Dow surged 3.0%
today, the strongest post-midterm rally since 1982. The S&P 500 jumped
1.6%, its best session in three months. The Nasdaq-100 advanced 0.9%.
The relief rally extended beyond equities. The 10-year Treasury yield fell
to 4.90%, back in what we have called the "old normal" range of
4.00%-5.00% (chart).

The dollar index (DXY) fell more than 1.5%, its biggest decline since
September. Gold rallied back above $4,000 an ounce. It might be finally
bottoming.

### Page 3

de-escalation in the Middle East
Brent crude plunged 8.7% to $94.62 a barrel amid hopes of Middle East
de-escalation. The nearby futures price of gasoline plunged to $2.73
(chart).
Investors may be warming up to the idea that some constraints on the AI
buildout could be healthy, as we have been arguing recently. Democratic
victories in key data center states, including Texas and Georgia, raise the
prospect of tighter construction restrictions amid concerns about electricity

and water consumption.
Hyperscalers rallied today, with Amazon and Microsoft gaining more than
6%, as capital discipline could ease concerns about AI investment returns
and improve free cash flow (chart). AI infrastructure providers struggled,

### Page 4

however. The PHLX Semiconductor Index fell 3.1% amid fears of weaker
demand from a slower buildout. We may be entering an AI Goldilocks
zone, with enough restraint to take some air out of the bubble without
derailing the boom.
II. The US Economy Continues To Perform Well
The economy and, therefore, earnings remain fundamentally resilient,
which continues to support the bull market. With the midterms behind us
and political uncertainty receding, investors can refocus on these strong

fundamentals. Here are the highlights of today's upbeat economic
indicators:
(1) ISM Services PMI. The ISM-NM PMI edged down modestly to 54.5 in
October, marking its 28th consecutive month of expansion (chart).
Underlying demand remained solid, with new orders and business activity
both comfortably above the 50.0 expansion threshold. Encouragingly, the
employment index rose from 50.1 to 51.4, marking its second consecutive
month of expansion.

### Page 5

Inflation is still a concern. The ISM prices-paid indexes remained elevated
in October (chart).
(2) Labor market. The average of the ISM employment indexes rose to
51.9 in October, its highest reading in roughly three years (chart). ADP
reported an 86,000 increase in private payrolls in October, beating
consensus expectations of 67,000.

### Page 6

(3) Trade balance. The latest trade data point to resilient US and global
economic growth. Merchandise imports rose 2.4% m/m in September to a
record $3.44 trillion annualized, while exports increased 2.5% m/m to
$2.09 trillion. The merchandise trade deficit widened to $1.35 trillion
annualized (chart).
Nominal and real US merchandise exports plus imports rose 21.1% y/y
and 10.1%, respectively, in September (chart). That suggests global trade
remains firmly in expansion territory and is consistent with strong global

economic growth.

### Page 7

Selected technology imports reached a record $61.3 billion in September,
reflecting the AI buildout’s heavy reliance on imported semiconductors,
servers, and other hardware (chart).

continue to be a source of volatility until time and data give
markets more certainty about where we land.
Meanwhile in bond markets, 10y Bund yields slid below 3.3%. The

10y OAT/Bund spread has eased below 150bp, tightening more
than 10bp on the day. We had pencilled in some spread tightening
towards the end of the year mainly on the expectation that a 2027
budget showing some deﬁcit consolidation will be passed, even if
only by decree. But beyond that, the uncertainty ahead of the
looming presidential election should leave spreads on wider levels
still.
Higher energy prices force a hike from the
Bank of England
With energy prices still elevated, we think inﬂation could reach 4%

early next year, which means a hike by the Bank of England (BoE) is
warranted. A hike today won’t come as a surprise, with 24bp
already priced in, but we are less convinced about the need for
more hikes thereafter. Markets take a more hawkish view and price
in almost three more hikes after today.
We don’t expect a signiﬁcant repricing of rates based on today’s
meeting, as much will depend on inﬂation data. Markets have
become less correlated with oil and we think each inﬂation release

in the coming months will be anxiously watched for second-round
eﬀects. We think inﬂation dynamics should start improving by mid-
2027, warranting the start of a big repricing lower for GBP rates.
Thursday’s events and market views
We start the day with French industrial production in
September, for which consensus pencils in -0.3% YoY growth.
Other interesting data includes eurozone PPIs for September,

Document 507: グラフで見る景気予報 (2026年10月)

Source PDF: all report pdf/グラフで見る景気予報 (2026年10月).pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 23
Status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 2 日

調査レポート

グラフで見る景気予報 (10 月)

調査部 
【今月の景気判断】 
国内景気は、米国とイランの対立が逆風となっているものの、政府の物価高対策や代替調達の進展などが下
支えとなり、緩やかに持ち直している。内需においては、物価高を背景に家計の節約志向が根強いものの、雇
用・所得環境の改善が下支えとなり、個人消費は緩やかに持ち直している。他方、企業部門では、投資意欲は良
好なものの、設備や建設資材価格の上昇を受けて、設備投資は横ばいとなっている。外需については、日中関
係の悪化によりインバウンド需要の増勢が鈍化しているものの、トランプ関税の影響で落ち込んでいた米国向け
輸出が持ち直していることなどから、全体としては底堅く推移している。こうした動きを受けて、生産にも持ち直し
の動きがみられる。 
今後は、供給制約懸念の後退や良好な雇用・所得環境、堅調な企業業績を背景に、個人消費や設備投資と
いった内需が持ち直すことで、景気は緩やかな持ち直しを続けると見込まれる。世界的な AI 関連需要の堅調さ
に支えられて、輸出が底堅く推移することも下支えとなるだろう。 
ただし、景気の下振れリスクも依然として残っている。米国とイランの対立が長期化する中、原油・石油製品価
格の高止まりや円安進行によるインフレの再加速、原油やナフサといった輸入原材料不足による供給制約の再
燃には注意が必要である。また、日中関係の悪化に伴うインバウンド需要の低迷や戦略物資の輸出規制の強化、
米国の通商政策をめぐる不透明感、金利の予期せぬ上昇による住宅投資や設備投資の抑制、人手不足の深刻
化、地震・天候不順なども、景気の下押し要因として意識される。

【今月の景気予報】

### Page 5

1. 景気全般 ～緩やかに持ち直している 
2026 年 4～6 月期の実質 GDP 成長率（2 次速報）は前期比＋0.4%（年率換算＋1.4%）と 3 四半期連続のプ
ラス成長となった。イラン情勢の悪化によって物価高の加速や供給制約などに対する懸念が強まったが、政府の
対策が下支えとなり、外需を中心に底堅く推移した。今後も緩やかな回復基調が続くと期待されるが、米・イラン
の対立長期化に伴う原油価格の高止まりや供給制約懸念の再燃による下押しリスクには警戒が必要だろう。

### Page 6

9 月調査の日銀短観における大企業の業況判断 DI は、製造業では、イラン情勢の悪化を受けた供給制約懸
念が和らぐ中、コスト増加分の価格転嫁が進んだことから、石油・石炭製品などを中心に改善した。一方、非製造
業はコスト増加や地震・長雨などが下押し要因となり、宿泊・飲食サービスなどを中心に悪化した。先行きは、米
国とイランの対立長期化リスクが意識され、製造業、非製造業ともに悪化と、慎重な見通しとなった。

日銀短観（業況判断 DI）

調査対象企業の見直しが行われたため、前後の系列にギャップがある。 
（出所）日本銀行「全国企業短期経済観測調査」

4～6 月期の企業業績（金融業・保険業を除く）は、売上高が前期比＋2.7%と 4 四半期連続で増加し、利益率
も前期から改善したため、経常利益は同＋11.0%と 4 四半期連続で増加した（業種別の経常利益は、製造業で
は前期比＋12.1%、非製造業では同＋10.2%）。今後も米国とイランの対立長期化による資源価格の高止まりな
どが下押し要因となるものの、国内景気の持ち直しに支えられて底堅く推移するとみられる。

### Page 7

7 月の CI 一致指数は前月差＋1.7 ポイントと 2 カ月連続で上昇した。発表済み 9 系列のうち 5 系列がプラス
に寄与した。中でも投資財出荷指数（除輸送機械）や商業販売額（小売業）のプラス寄与が大きかった。8月のCI
一致指数は鉱工業用生産財出荷指数や耐久消費財出荷指数のマイナス寄与が大きく、3 カ月ぶりに下落すると
見込まれる。基調判断は「改善」が維持されよう。

景気動向指数（CI・DI）

（出所）内閣府「景気動向指数」

8 月の景気ウォッチャー調査の現状判断 DI（季節調整値）は 46.4 と 4 カ月連続で上昇した（前月差＋0.7 ポ
イント）。家計動向関連は、住宅価格や金利の先高観が一部で需要を喚起しているとの声を受けて住宅関連を中
心に改善した。企業動向関連は、好調な観光需要を受けて非製造業を中心に改善した。先行き判断DI（季節調
整値）は 5 カ月連続で上昇しており、イラン情勢が最悪期を脱出しつつあるとの見方がマインドを下支えしている。

景気ウォッチャー調査（季節調整値）

（注）景気の現状判断 DI は、家計動向関連業種、企業動向関連業種、雇用関連業種の景気ウォッチャ
ーによる景気判断から算出される。50 を上回ると「景気が良くなっている」、50 を下回ると「景気が
悪くなっている」という判断が優勢であることを示す。

### Page 8

2. 生産 ～持ち直しの動き 
8 月の鉱工業生産は前月比－1.7%と 2 カ月連続で減少した。生産用機械工業などは増加した一方、自動車
工業や汎用・業務用機械工業などが減少し、全体を押し下げた。製造業の生産活動には持ち直しの動きがみら
れる。製造工業生産予測調査では 9 月は前月比＋3.2%（同調査をもとにした経済産業省の予測は同＋1.6%）、
10 月は同＋3.1%の計画だが、米・イランの対立長期化や相次ぐ自然災害の影響などで下振れる可能性がある。

8 月の鉱工業出荷は、自動車工業や輸送機械工業（除く自動車工業）などを中心に前月比－2.5%と 2 カ月ぶ
りに減少した。また、前年比では生産用機械工業などを中心に＋1.9%と 3 カ月連続で増加した。在庫は石油・石
炭製品工業や自動車工業などを中心に前月比－0.5%と 3 カ月ぶりに減少した。また、前年比では電気・情報通

方、広義対事業所サービスは低下した。具体的には、金融業・保険業などは低下したものの、生活娯楽関連サー
ビスや小売業などが上昇し、全体を押し上げた。均してみれば、緩やかに上昇している。今後も国内景気の持ち
直しに併せて上昇傾向で推移すると期待されるが、物価高などを受けて下振れるリスクもある。

### Page 10

3. 雇用 ～改善している 
8 月の完全失業率は 2.5%と低水準で推移した。就業者（前月差＋3 万人）の増加は小幅にとどまる一方で失
業者（同＋10 万人）が増加したが、労働市場への新規流入増で労働力人口（同＋11 万人）が増加したことが原
因であり、労働需給のタイトな状態に変化はない。人件費の増加などから中小企業を中心に採用が慎重化してい
る可能性があるが、それでも人手不足感は依然として強く、雇用情勢は良好な状態を維持しよう。

失業率と就業者数

（出所）総務省「労働力調査」

8 月の有効求人倍率は前月と同じ 1.18 倍となった。有効求人数（前月比＋0.7%）、有効求職者数（同＋0.7%）
とも小幅増加した。新規求人倍率は 2.06 倍に低下した。新規求人数（同－1.5%）が減少する一方で、新規求職
申込件数（同＋0.6%）が増加したことで、2021 年 11 月以来の水準まで低下した。人手不足は深刻化しているが、
賃金上昇を背景に企業は新規採用に慎重になっている可能性があり、有効求人倍率は緩やかに低下していこう。

有効求人倍率と新規求人倍率

### Page 11

4. 賃金 ～増加している 
7 月の一人当たり現金給与総額は前年比＋4.3%と堅調な増加が続いている。所定内給与（同＋3.8%）、所定
外給与（同＋4.5%）に加え、夏のボーナスを含む特別給与（同＋5.3 %）も堅調に増加した。また、足元の物価上
昇率鈍化もあり、実質賃金は同＋2.0%と 7 カ月連続でプラスとなった。雇用情勢の良好な状態を背景に今後も
賃金の増加が維持されるうえ、目先は政策効果で物価安定が予想され、実質賃金はプラス圏での推移が続こう。

現金給与総額と所定内・所定外給与

（注）現金給与総額、所定内給与は名目賃金指数から、所定外給与は月間現金給与額から計算  
（出所）厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計」

5. 個人消費 ～緩やかに持ち直している 
7 月の二人以上世帯の実質消費支出は前月比＋0.5%と増加した。また日本の世帯全体の消費動向を表す 7
月の総消費動向指数（実質）は横ばい となった。基調としては、個人消費は緩やかに持ち直している。実質雇用
者報酬は引き続き緩やかな増加を続けているのに加え、消費者態度指数からみた消費者マインドは持ち直して
いることから、個人消費は当面、緩やかな持ち直しが続こう。

実質消費支出（二人以上の世帯）と総消費動向指数（実質）

8 月の商業販売額（小売業）は前年比＋2.7%と 6 カ月連続で増加した。内訳をみると、自動車小売業、各種商
品小売業、飲食料品小売業、医薬品・化粧品小売業などは増加した一方、織物・衣服・身の回り品小売業、機械
器具小売業、燃料小売業は減少した。季節調整値は前月比－1.2%と 2 カ月ぶりに減少した。9 月の新車販売台
数（乗用車、除く軽自動車）は前年比＋3.0%と増加した。

個人消費関連指標

（出所）日本自動車販売協会連合会「自動車国内販売」、経済産業省「商業動態統計」

8 月の消費者態度指数（二人以上の世帯、季節調整値）は前月差＋0.6 ポイントと 4 カ月連続で上昇した。内
訳をみると、「雇用環境」、「収入の増え方」は低下した一方、「暮らし向き」、「耐久消費財の買い時判断」は上昇
が続いた。消費者態度指数からみた消費者マインドは、このところ持ち直しており、良好な雇用・所得環境の下、
今後も持ち直しが続くと期待される。

### Page 13

6. 住宅投資 ～横ばい 
4～6 月期の住宅投資（実質 GDP ベース、2 次速報）は前期比－0.6%と 3 四半期ぶりに減少した。8 月の住
宅着工戸数は前年比＋6.1%と 5 カ月連続で増加した。季節調整済年率換算値では75.3 万戸（前月比－2.7%）
と 2 カ月連続で減少した。均してみれば横ばいとなっている。今後は金利上昇やイラン情勢悪化を受けた建設資
材価格の上昇などが下押し要因となる一方、所得環境の改善が下支えとなり、横ばい圏で推移すると見込まれる。

住宅着工

（出所）国土交通省「建築着工統計」

7. 設備投資 ～横ばい 
4～6 月期の設備投資（実質 GDP ベース、2 次速報）は前期比－0.9%と 2 四半期連続で減少した。設備投資
に先行する機械受注（船舶・電力を除く民需）は、7 月は前月比－3.7%と 2 カ月ぶりに減少した。うち製造業は前
月比－1.0%、非製造業（船舶・電力を除く）は同－2.6%といずれも減少した。このところ設備投資は横ばいとなっ
ているが、投資意欲は良好であることから、今後は持ち直しへ向かうと期待される。

機械受注と設備投資

### Page 14

8. 公共投資 ～横ばい圏で推移している 
4～6 月期の公共投資（実質 GDP ベース、2 次速報）は、前期比－0.5%と 2 四半期ぶりに減少したが、均して
みれば横ばい圏で推移している。公共投資の先行指標である公共工事請負額は、8 月は地方からが減少し、全
体では前年比－2.4%と 3 カ月ぶりに減少した。工事コストの増加を受けた公共投資デフレーターの上昇によって
実質値の伸びが抑制される状況が続いており、今後も公共投資は横ばい圏で推移する可能性がある。

公共投資と公共工事請負額

（注）国は独立行政法人などを含む、地方は都道府県、市区町村、地方公社の合計

    西日本建設業保証「公共工事前払金保証統計」

9. 輸出入・国際収支 ～輸出、輸入ともに横ばい 
8 月の実質輸出は前月比－0.8%と 4 カ月ぶりに減少した。均してみれば、横ばいとなっている。好調な AI 関
連需要が下支えとなる中、イラン情勢悪化の影響が緩和へ向かうことで、持ち直しへ向かうと期待される。また、 8
月の実質輸入は前月比＋1.6%と 4 カ月連続で増加した。均してみれば、横ばいとなっている。当面、中東からの
輸入が伸び悩む一方、代替輸入の増加が見込まれることから、横ばい圏で推移する可能性がある。

7 月の経常収支（季節調整値）は 2 兆 5,231 億円の黒字となった。第二次所得収支の赤字幅は拡大したもの

### Page 17

10. 物価 ～企業物価は急上昇、消費者物価は緩やかに上昇 
8 月の国内企業物価は前年比＋ 7.6%と、電力・都市ガス・水道などを中心に前年比でプラスが続いている。   
一方、8 月の消費者物価は、「生鮮食品を除く総合」が前年比＋1.7 %、「生鮮食品及びエネルギーを除く総合」
が同＋1.9%と、緩やかな上昇が続いている。政府による種々の物価高対策により当面は安定した推移が見込ま
れるものの、原油価格の上昇が企業のコスト転嫁を通じて物価の上振れにつながる可能性がある。

国内物価

（出所）日本銀行「企業物価指数」、総務省「消費者物価指数月報」

11. 世界景気 ～米国は堅調、欧州も底堅いが、中国は弱い 
世界景気は米国は堅調で、欧州も底堅いが、中国は弱い動きが続く。4～6 月期の実質 GDP 成長率は、米国
は前期比年率＋2.2%に鈍化したが、個人消費、設備投資は底堅く推移し、民間最終需要は約 3 年ぶりの高い
伸びを記録した。欧州は同＋2.6%に加速し 4 年ぶりの高い伸びとなった。一方、中国は前年比＋4.3%に鈍化し
3 年半ぶりの低い伸びにとどまった。先行き、米国は底堅いものの欧州、中国は弱い動きが続く見込みである。

米国・欧州・中国の実質 GDP 成長率

（出所）米商務省、欧州連合統計局、中国国家統計局

12. 世界の物価 ～原油価格上昇の影響が懸念材料 
世界の消費者物価上昇率は、米国、欧州ではエネルギー価格などを中心に高水準で推移している。米国は 8
月に前年比＋3.4%と高水準で横ばいとなった。ユーロ圏は 8 月に同＋ 3.2%に加速した。 中国も 8 月に同           
＋0.8%と小幅に加速した。先行き、原油価格上昇の影響により、世界の消費者物価上昇率は一段と上昇する見
込みである。

米国・欧州・中国の消費者物価上昇率

（出所）米労働省、欧州連合統計局、中国国家統計局 
13. 原油 ～上昇後、やや下落 
9 月のブレント原油は上昇後、やや下落した。前半は、米・イランの攻撃の応酬が再燃したことを受けて、約 4
カ月ぶりの高値をつけた。後半は、「東西パイプライン」への攻撃などから落ち込んだサウジアラビアからの原油
供給が回復するとの期待などから、やや下落する場面があった。米国とイランとの交渉における駆け引きや、フー
シ派によるサウジアラビアへの攻撃は続いており、原油相場は中東情勢を材料に不安定な推移となろう。

原油価格の推移

14. 国内金利～上昇 
9 月の長期金利（新発 10 年国債利回り）は上昇。物価上昇圧力の高まりを背景に日銀の利上げペースが加速
するとの観測、高市政権の財政拡張による国債の需給悪化懸念の台頭、米金利の上昇などを受けて月初から長
期金利は上昇基調が続き、25 日には一時 3.115%と 1996 年 8 月以来、約 30 年ぶりの高水準を付けた。財政
悪化と物価上昇への警戒感、日銀の追加利上げ観測、米金利の上昇から、当面金利は上昇しやすい。

国内金利

（出所）日本銀行

15. 米国金利 ～上昇 
9 月の米長期金利は上昇。財務省が国債の買い戻し上限額を最大 60 億ドルに拡大すると発表したものの規
模が不十分との見方が広がったこと、原油価格が上昇しインフレ警戒感 が強まったことに加え、16 日の FOMC
（米連邦公開市場委員会）での利上げ後も金融引き締めが続くとの観測から上昇が続き、月末には 5.3%台と
2007 年以来約 19 年ぶりの高水準を付けた。追加利上げ観測を背景に金利が上昇しやすい地合いが続こう。

16. 国内株価～月末にかけて反発 
9 月の日経平均株価は下落後、月末にかけて反発。世界的な金利上昇、中東情勢悪化を受けた原油高、円
の反発、AI 開発を減速させるべきとの意見の米国での広がりを受けて、中旬にかけて値がさの AI・半導体関連
株を中心に下落した。ただし、シルバーウイーク中の米国市場での株高、原油価格の反落を受けて、月末にかけ
て反発した。AI・半導体関連株を中心に当面荒っぽい値動きが続こう。

日本株価

（出所）日本経済新聞

17. 米国株価～下落 
9 月の米国株価は下落。長期金利上昇から月初より軟調な推移となり、米 AI 開発企業のトップらが AI 開発の
減速を呼び掛けたこと、16 日の米連邦準備制度理事会（FRB）による利上げ、ウォーシュ議長が記者会見でイン
フレの根強さを強調したことなどが株価の重荷となった。米中首脳会談も特段の材料とならず、月末にかけても金
利上昇を受けて弱含んだ。金利上昇、物価高懸念を受けて、当面荒っぽい値動きが続こう。

米国株価

### Page 21

18. 為替～円は急騰後、反落 
9 月の円は対米ドルで急騰後、反落。円は月初に 1 ドル=160 円近辺まで弱含んだが、ベッセント米財務長官
による日銀に早期利上げを促す発言、日銀の利上げペースが加速するとの観測から、投機筋の円売りポジション
の解消の動きも加わって、8 日には 152 円 80 銭台まで上昇した。もっともその後は、原油価格上昇や米長期金
利上昇などを受けて円は下落に転じ、16 日の米国の利上げ、ウォーシュ FRB 議長の記者会見でのタカ派的な
発言を受け 156 円台まで下落した。さらに 18 日の日銀金融政策決定会合では、市場の予想通り 0.25%の利上
げが決定されたが、2 名の審議委員が反対するなど今後の追加利上げに不透明感が強まったことで円買い材料
とならず、連休明け後には米金利上昇を受けて 159 円台まで下落した。25 日に片山財務相が先の日米首脳会
談で「トランプ米大統領から円安への懸念が示された」と発言したことなどで円は一時 156 円台まで反発したが、
円買いの勢いは続かなかった。積極的な円買い材料に乏しく、原油価格の上昇や米金利の上昇が続けば、円は
さらに弱含む可能性がある。

### Page 22

19. 金融～マネーストックは緩やかに増加し、銀行貸出残高は堅調に増加 
日銀は 2026 年 9 月の金融政策決定会合において、基調的な物価上昇率が 2%の物価安定目標を超えて上
振れしていくリスクがあるとして、無担保コールレート（オーバーナイト物）の目標水準を 1.25%に引き上げた（今
次利上げ局面では 2024 年 7 月、2025 年 1 月、12 月、2026 年 6 月に続き 5 回目）。また、中東情勢悪化を受
けた原油価格上昇、AI 関連需要の増加に伴う半導体価格などの上昇、為替円安による耐久財などの価格上昇
により、物価の上昇ペースが速まることへの懸念を強めており、引き続き政策金利を引き上げる姿勢を示している。
金融を巡る動きとしては、8 月マネーストック（M2）は前年比＋2.0%と緩やかに増加している一方、8 月銀行貸出
残高は同＋5.8%と堅調に増加している。

マネーストックと銀行貸出

景気予報ガイド 
【今月の景気判断】 
○レポート発表時における景気判断。【今月の景気予報】における各項目の現況をもとに総合的に判断。

【今月の景気予報】 
○3 カ月前～・・・3 カ月前～現況の「方向感」を、①改善、②横ばい、③悪化の 3 段階に分類。 
○現況 ・・・・・各項目の現在の「水準」を、①晴れ、②曇り、③雨の 3 段階に分類。 
○～3 カ月後・・・現況～3 カ月後の「方向感」を、①改善、②横ばい、③悪化の 3 段階に分類。

（注）現況をあらわす天気マークはあくまで「水準」を判断したもので、3 カ月前～の矢印が①改善、③悪化の場合で
も、必ずしも天気マークが変更されるわけではない。

【当面の注目材料】 
○各項目の現況、見通しをふまえて、今後景気を判断する上で注目すべきポイント。

【各項目のヘッドライン】 
○全 19 項目について、毎月簡単なヘッドラインを記載、当月と前月のものを対比して示している。 
○右端は各項目のグラフが掲載されているページ数。

【前月からの変更点】 
○景気予報において判断の変更があった場合に変更理由を記載。 
○ヘッドラインに記載されている市場データ（原油・金利・株価・為替）については記載せず。

【主要経済指標の推移】 
○当レポートで用いられている経済指標の半年間の動きを掲載。

[ニュージーランド]～雇用環境が厳しさを増すなか、７-９月期の失業率は 11 年ぶりの高水準に～ 
４日に発表された７-９月の失業率（季調済）は5.7％となり、前期（5.6％）から 0.1pt 悪化し
て 2015 年７-９月以来、11 年ぶりの高水準となった。 失業者数は前期比＋0.3 万人と前期 （同＋0.8
万人）に続いて２四半期連続で増加しており、中期的にも増加基調が続く動きが確認されている。
一方、 就業者数は前期比▲0.5 万人と前期 （同＋1.0 万人） から５四半期ぶりの減少に転じており、
持ち直しの動きに一服感が出ている。 業種別では、 世界的なＡＩ （人工知能） ・半導体関連需要の旺
盛さを追い風とするデータセンター関連投資の堅調さが建設業の雇用を下支えする一方、 製造業で
は雇用拡大の動きに一服感がみられるとともに、 サービス業でも幅広い業種で頭打ちの動きが確認

### Page 2

2/5 
されている。雇用環境の悪化を受けて労働力人口も前期比▲0.2 万人と前期（同＋1.8 万人）から
５四半期ぶりの減少に転じており、 労働参加率も低下するなど、 労働市場への参加の動きが弱まっ
ている。さらに、賃金の伸びも鈍化しており、イラン情勢の悪化に伴うエネルギー価格の上昇がイ
ンフレ圧力を強めるなか、家計の実質購買力が下押しされるなど、 個人消費を取り巻く環境は厳し
さを増している。

[韓国]～AI･半導体需要を追い風に輸出の拡大が続く一方、サービス物価を中心にインフレ圧力くすぶる～ 
１日に発表された 10 月の輸出額は前年同月比＋93.7％となり、前月（同＋83.7％）から伸びが
加速した。 当研究所が試算した季節調整値に基づく前月比もプラスとなっており、 旺盛な動きが続
いている。 財別では、 世界的なＡＩ （人工知能） ・半導体関連需要の旺盛さを反映して、 主力輸出財
である半導体関連の輸出が引き続き堅調に推移するとともに、 コンピュータ関連の輸出も大幅に拡
大している。国・地域別でも、最大の輸出先である中国向けのほか、米国向けやＥＵ（欧州連合）
向け、ASEAN（東南アジア諸国連合）をはじめとするアジア新興国向けも軒並み大幅に拡大してい
る。一方、輸入額は前年同月比＋36.0％となり、前月（同＋26.0％）から伸びが加速した。前月比
も小幅ながら２ヵ月連続で増加したと試算されるなど、 持ち直しの動きが続いている。 輸出の堅調
さを反映して半導体製造装置の輸入が押し上げられたほか、 素材・部材関連の輸入も堅調に推移す
るとともに、 原油などの商品市況の上昇も輸入額を押し上げている。 その結果、 貿易収支は＋425.01
億ドルと前月（＋499.72 億ドル）から黒字幅が縮小した。 
３日に発表された 10 月の消費者物価は前年同月比＋2.8％となり、前月（同＋2.9％）からわず
かに伸びが鈍化した。前月比も＋0.25％と前月（同＋0.32％）から上昇ペースが鈍化しており、原
油価格の高止まりを受けてエネルギー価格は上昇基調が続く一方、 生鮮食品をはじめとする食料品
価格の上昇に一服感がみられるなど、生活必需品の物価動向にばらつきが生じている。なお、食料
品とエネルギーを除いたコアインフレ率は前年同月比＋2.8％となり、前月（同＋2.8％）から横ば
いで推移しており、総合インフレ率とともに中銀目標（２％）を上回る水準にある。前月比は＋
0.39％と前月（同▲0.09％）から２ヵ月ぶりの上昇に転じており、エネルギー価格の上昇に伴い輸
送コストに押し上げ圧力がかかる一方、 金融市場におけるウォン相場の強含みを受けて輸入物価の
上昇が抑えられるなど、財価格は比較的落ち着いた動きをみせている。他方、コスト上昇分の価格
転嫁を反映してサービス価格を中心に上昇する動きが確認されており、 基調的なインフレ圧力は依

[インドネシア]～インフレ率は鈍化するも物価上昇圧力は根強く、輸出は過去最高を更新するなど堅調～ 
１日に発表された 10 月の消費者物価は前年同月比＋3.23％となり、前月（同＋3.28％）からわ
ずかに伸びが鈍化した。前月比は＋0.22％と前月（同＋0.30％）から上昇ペースこそ鈍化したもの
の、３ヵ月連続で上昇している。エネルギー価格が２ヵ月ぶりの下落に転じる一方、生鮮食品をは
じめとする食料品価格は上昇が続いており、 生活必需品の物価動向にはばらつきがみられる。 なお、
コアインフレ率も前年同月比＋2.56％となり、前月（同＋2.84％）から伸びが鈍化しており、総合
インフレ率とともに中銀の目標レンジ （2.5±１％） 内に収まっている。 一方、 前月比は＋0.12％と
前月（同＋0.10％）からわずかに上昇ペースが加速しており、輸送コストへの上昇圧力に加え、金
融市場におけるルピア安を受けた輸入物価の押し上げ圧力も重なり、 幅広い財の価格が上昇する動
きがみられる。 さらに、 コスト上昇分の価格転嫁を反映してサービス価格も上昇基調で推移するな
ど、基調的なインフレ圧力は依然として根強い様子がうかがえる。 
また、 同日に発表された９月の輸出額は前年同月比＋11.87％となり、 前月 （同＋6.72％） から伸
びが加速した。 当研究所が試算した季節調整値に基づく前月比も３ヵ月ぶりのプラスに転じており、
単月ベースの輸出額は過去最高を更新している。 財別では、 同国政府による事実上の輸出規制策を
受けて天然ガスの輸出額が下振れするとともに、原油の輸出額も頭打ちしている。一方、鉱物資源
関連や農産品の輸出額が底堅い動きをみせているほか、製造業関連の輸出も堅調に推移しており、
輸出全体を押し上げている。他方、輸入額は前年同月比＋18.41％となり、前月（同＋19.09％）か
ら伸びが鈍化した。 ただし、 当研究所が試算した季節調整値に基づく前月比は３ヵ月ぶりのプラス
に転じており、 持ち直しの動きがみられる。 原油や石油製品関連の輸入額が堅調に推移するととも
に、機械製品関連の輸入も底堅い動きをみせている。その結果、貿易収支は＋33.57 億ドルと前月
（＋35.52 億ドル）から黒字幅が縮小した。

[台湾]～インフレ率、コアインフレ率ともに伸びは鈍化も、幅広い品目でインフレ圧力が強まる動き～ 
５日に発表された 10 月の消費者物価は前年同月比＋2.33％となり、前月（同＋2.74％）から伸
びが鈍化した。前月比も＋0.06％と前月（同＋0.45％）から上昇ペースが鈍化しており、電力料金
の据え置きによりエネルギー価格の上昇に一服感がみられるとともに、 生鮮食品をはじめとする食
料品価格が下落に転じるなど、生活必需品を中心に物価上昇圧力が和らいだことが影響している。
生鮮食料品とエネルギーを除いたコアインフレ率も前年同月比＋2.23％となり、 前月 （同＋2.53％）
から伸びが鈍化した。一方、前月比は＋0.54％と前月（同＋0.07％）から上昇ペースが加速してい
る。 ガソリン価格の上昇などに伴う輸送コストの増加に加え、 金融市場における台湾ドル安を受け
た輸入物価の押し上げ圧力も重なり、 幅広い財の価格が上昇しているほか、 コスト上昇分の価格転
嫁を反映してサービス価格も上昇するなど、インフレ圧力が広がる動きがみられる。

[タイ]～生活必需品にとどまらず幅広い品目で物価が上昇し、インフレ率は中銀の目標レンジを上回る～

を上回った。前月比も＋0.94％と前月（同＋0.25％）から上昇ペースが加速した。背景には、原油
価格の再上昇を受けてエネルギー価格が上昇したことに加え、 生鮮食品をはじめとする食料品価格
も上昇するなど、 生活必需品を中心にインフレ圧力が強まったことがある。食料品とエネルギーを
除いたコアインフレ率も前年同月比＋1.58％となり、 引き続き中銀の目標レンジに相当する水準に
とどまっているものの、前月（同＋1.50％）から伸びが加速して 2023 年４月以来の高い伸びとな

[フィリピン]～インフレ率、コアインフレ率ともに伸びが加速し、中銀の目標レンジを上回る水準で推移～ 
５日に発表された 10 月の消費者物価は前年同月比＋7.8％となり、前月（同＋7.2％）から伸び
が加速して 2023 年２月以来の高い伸びとなった。前月比は＋0.65％と前月（同＋0.95％）から上

比＋5.4％となり、 前月 （同＋4.8％） から伸びが加速して2023 年９月以来の高い伸びとなるなど、
総合インフレ率とともに中銀の目標レンジ（２～４％）の上限を上回る水準にある。前月比も＋

Source PDF: all report pdf/中信建投武超则领衔未来产业研究院带你拥抱“确定性”，把握未来产业投资机遇.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 9
